# Rulings — the scheduler and amendments: the full rows

**Never loaded by itself — searched** (D390). Each row's one-line short form is in `.claude/rules/decisions/scheduler.md`, which is what a
chat loads. Open a ruling's full row before acting on its detail or asking him about it:
`grep -h '^| D149 |' .claude/decisions-full/*.md` (the shell — the Grep tool hides a long row). A mark on an older ruling
(DECISIONS.md step 2) is written HERE, in its full row; `backlog-archive.mjs --rulings` then refreshes its short line's
change tail. Newest first; each row keeps the date it was recorded.

| # | Date | His ruling, in his words where short enough | What it means | Where it lives now |

| D47 | 22 Sep 26 | *“Keep the refusal”* — asked whether a placeholder puck on a JET seat should be refused outright or merely warned about, after being shown that the two options differ in where the bugs land | **D33 stands whole, and it beats the 13 AUG 26 “placeholder puck is a shortcut” rule on cockpit seats only.** ALL and ALL AVAIL are refused on flying-line cockpit seats, with the reason on screen at every door. **The 13 Aug shortcut is NOT retired** — a placeholder puck still arms its seat everywhere a placeholder is legal (duty desks, sim seats and passengers, ground rows, the Common Programme, an accepted request row), and tapping an EMPTY cockpit seat still narrows the crew list as it always did. Only the cockpit instance of the shortcut is gone, because the puck can no longer get there. **What settled it:** the two lists are not the same question. Tapping an empty seat asks “who can fly THIS seat” — seat, qualification, SC currency, crew rest, busy, absences. The puck asks only “who has nothing else on at that time”, so on a front seat it would include WSOs, men not SC current and men who would break crew rest — and would then pay every one of them for a sortie they could not have flown. The two also use different windows (availability step→dekit; the flying rule report→debrief, D36). **The risk argument he asked for:** a refusal removes the state, so roughly ten downstream readers never have to handle it, and its failure mode is LOUD (a placeholder blocked where it should be allowed). A warning keeps the state, needs a cockpit branch in every one of those readers, and its failure mode is SILENT and about money. **Not covered, and belted separately:** a placeholder arriving from a copied day template, a parked plan or older saved data bypasses both doors — a jet seat credits nobody whatever sits in it. | `raptor-port/docs/ui-contracts.md` §Arm-and-plant; `raptor-port/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-22-oil-seats-can-earn-plan.md` §2 (clash list); pinned by `raptor-port/src/ui/interact.test.tsx` and `src/ui/oilseat-refusal.test.tsx` |
